/data/results/claude/set_a/post_research/4/security/src/main/java/com/example/accounts/PasswordHasher.java =====\n     1\tpackage com.example.accounts;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.nio.ByteBuffer;\n     4\timport java.nio.CharBuffer;\n     5\timport java.nio.charset.CharacterCodingException;\n     6\timport java.nio.charset.CharsetEncoder;\n     7\timport java.nio.charset.CodingErrorAction;\n     8\timport java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;\n     9\timport java.security.MessageDigest;\n    10\timport java.security.SecureRandom;\n    11\timport java.util.Arrays;\n    12\timport java.util.Base64;\n    13\timport java.util.Locale;\n    14\timport org.bouncycastle.crypto.generators.Argon2BytesGenerator;\n    15\timport org.bouncycastle.crypto.params.Argon2Parameters;\n    16\t\n    17\t/**\n    18\t * Argon2id password hashing, stored in PHC string format.\n    19\t *\n    20\t * <p>Rule 8 (prefer the platform): Argon2id is the current OWASP first choice for password storage\n    21\t * and this class does not invent any of it -- the KDF is Bouncy Castle's reviewed implementation,\n    22\t * the salt comes from {@link SecureRandom}, the comparison is {@link MessageDigest#isEqual} and the\n    23\t * on-disk encoding is the standard PHC string, so another tool can verify the same hashes. The JDK\n    24\t * ships no Argon2, which is the only reason a third-party dependency appears at all.\n    25\t *\n    26\t * <p>Rule 1 (trust boundaries): a stored hash is a value read back from a database, i.e. untrusted\n    27\t * input. {@link #verify} therefore parses it strictly and rejects out-of-range cost parameters,\n    28\t * rather than obediently allocating whatever {@code m=} a tampered row asks for.\n    29\t *\n    30\t * <p>Rule 5: the plaintext is handled as {@code char[]}, encoded through a {@link CharBuffer} so no\n    31\t * {@code String} copy is created, and every intermediate buffer is zeroed in a {@code finally}.\n    32\t */\n    33\tpublic final class PasswordHasher {\n    34\t\n    35\t    private static final String ALGORITHM_ID = \"argon2id\";\n    36\t    private static final int VERSION = Argon2Parameters.ARGON2_VERSION_13; // 0x13 == 19\n    37\t    private static final int SALT_LENGTH = 16;\n    38\t    private static final int HASH_LENGTH = 32;\n    39\t\n    40\t    /** Bounds applied when parsing a stored hash, independent of the configured cost. */\n    41\t    private static final int MIN_MEMORY_KIB = 8;\n    42\t    private static final int MAX_MEMORY_KIB = 1_048_576;   // 1 GiB ceiling\n    43\t    private static final int MAX_ITERATIONS = 20;\n    44\t    private static final int MAX_PARALLELISM = 16;\n    45\t    private static final int MAX_ENCODED_LENGTH = 512;\n    46\t\n    47\t    private static final Base64.Encoder B64 = Base64.getEncoder().withoutPadding();\n    48\t    private static final Base64.Decoder B64D = Base64.getDecoder();\n    49\t\n    50\t    private final Config.Argon2Settings settings;\n    51\t    private final byte[] pepper;\n    52\t    private final SecureRandom random;\n    53\t\n    54\t    /**\n    55\t     * @param pepper optional site-wide secret mixed in as Argon2's \"secret\" parameter; an empty\n    56\t     *               array means \"not configured\". Never logged, never persisted with the hash.\n    57\t     */\n    58\t    public PasswordHasher(Config.Argon2Settings settings, byte[] pepper) {\n    59\t        this.settings = settings;\n    60\t        this.pepper = pepper == null ? new byte[0] : pepper.clone();\n    61\t        this.random = new SecureRandom();\n    62\t    }\n    63\t\n    64\t    /** Hashes {@code password} with a fresh random salt. The caller still owns {@code password}. */\n    65\t    public String hash(char[] password) {\n    66\t        if (password == null || password.length == 0) {\n    67\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"password must not be empty\");\n    68\t        }\n    69\t        byte[] salt = new byte[SALT_LENGTH];\n    70\t        random.nextBytes(salt);\n    71\t        byte[] digest = derive(password, salt, settings.memoryKib(), settings.iterations(),\n    72\t                settings.parallelism());\n    73\t        try {\n    74\t            return encode(settings.memoryKib(), settings.iterations(), settings.parallelism(),\n    75\t                    salt, digest);\n    76\t        } finally {\n    77\t            Wipe.bytes(digest);\n    78\t        }\n    79\t    }\n    80\t\n    81\t    /**\n    82\t     * Verifies {@code password} against a stored PHC string.\n    83\t     *\n    84\t     * @return {@code true} only on a match; {@code false} for a mismatch <em>and</em> for any\n    85\t     *         unparsable or out-of-policy stored value -- a corrupt row fails closed (rule 3).\n    86\t     */\n    87\t    public boolean verify(char[] password, String encoded) {\n    88\t        if (password == null || password.length == 0 || encoded == null) {\n    89\t            return false;\n    90\t        }\n    91\t        Parsed parsed;\n    92\t        try {\n    93\t            parsed = parse(encoded);\n    94\t        } catch (IllegalArgumentException e) {\n    95\t            return false;\n    96\t        }\n    97\t        byte[] actual = null;\n    98\t        try {\n    99\t            actual = derive(password, parsed.salt, parsed.memoryKib, parsed.iterations,\n   100\t                    parsed.parallelism);\n   101\t            // Constant-time: a timing difference here would leak the hash prefix byte by byte.\n   102\t            return MessageDigest.isEqual(actual, parsed.hash);\n   103\t        } finally {\n   104\t            Wipe.bytes(actual);\n   105\t            Wipe.bytes(parsed.salt);\n   106\t            Wipe.bytes(parsed.hash);\n   107\t        }\n   108\t    }\n   109\t\n   110\t    /** True when {@code encoded} is valid but weaker than the current settings (rehash on login). */\n   111\t    public boolean needsRehash(String encoded) {\n   112\t        try {\n   113\t            Parsed parsed = parse(encoded);\n   114\t            try {\n   115\t                return parsed.memoryKib < settings.memoryKib()\n   116\t                        || parsed.iterations < settings.iterations()\n   117\t                        || parsed.hash.length < HASH_LENGTH\n   118\t                        || parsed.salt.length < SALT_LENGTH;\n   119\t 124\t            return true;\n   125\t        }\n   126\t    }\n   127\t\n   128\t    /**\n   129\t     * A throwaway hash with the current parameters, used to spend the same CPU on a login for an\n   130\t     * account that does not exist as on one that does (rule 6: comparable work for every failure).\n   131\t     */\n   132\t    public String newDecoyHash() {\n   133\t        byte[] secret = new byte[32];\n   134\t        random.nextBytes(secret);\n   135\t        char[] asChars = new char[secret.length];\n   136\t        try {\n   137\t            for (int i = 0; i < secret.length; i++) Wipe.bytes(secret);\n   143\t            Wipe.chars(asChars);\n   144\t        }\n   145\t    }\n   146\t\n   147\t    private byte[] derive(char[] password, byte[] salt, int memoryKib, int iterations,\n   148\t                          int parallelism) {\n   149\t        byte[] passwordBytes = toUtf8(password);\n   150\t        try {\n   151\t            Argon2Parameters.Builder builder =\n   152\t                    new Argon2Parameters.Builder(Argon2Parameters.ARGON2_id)\n   153\t                            .withVersion(VERSION)\n   154\t                            .withSalt(salt)\n   155\t                            .withMemoryAsKB(memoryKib)\n   156\t                            .withIterations(iterations)\n   157\t                            .withParallelism(parallelism);\n   158\t            if (pepper.length > 0) {\n   159\t                builder.withSecret(pepper);\n   160\t            }\n   161\t            Argon2BytesGenerator generator.generateBytes(passwordBytes, out);\n   165\t            return out;\n   166\t        } finally {\n   167\t            Wipe.bytes(passwordBytes);\n   168\t        }\n   169\t    }\n   170\t\n   171\t    /** UTF-8 encodes a {@code char[]} without creating an unwipeable {@code String}. */\n   172\t    private static byte[] toUtf8(char[] password) {\n   173\t        CharsetEncoder encoder = StandardCharsets.UTF_8.newEncoder()\n   174\t                .onMalformedInput(CodingErrorAction.REPORT)\n   175\t                .onUnmappableCharacter(CodingErrorAction.REPORT);\n   176\t        ByteBuffer buffer;\n   177\t        try {\n   178\t            buffer = encoder.encode(CharBuffer.wrap(password));\n   179\t        } catch (CharacterCodingException e) {\n   180\t            // 183\t        byte[] bytes = new byte[buffer.remaining()];\n   184\t        buffer.get(bytes);\n   185\t        if (buffer.hasArray()) {\n   186\t            Arrays.fill(buffer.array(), (byte) 0);\n   187\t        }\n   188\t        return bytes;\n   189\t    }\n   190\t\n   191\t    private static String encode(int memoryKib, int iterations, int parallelism, byte[] salt,\n   192\t                                 byte[] hash) {\n   193\t        return \"$\" + ALGORITHM_ID + \"$v=\" + VERSION\n   194\t                + \"$m=\" + memoryKib + \",t=\" + iterations + \",p=\" + parallelism\n   195\t Parsed(int memoryKib, int iterations, int parallelism, byte[] salt, byte[] hash) {\n   207\t            this.memoryKib = memoryKib;\n   208\t            this.iterations = iterations;\n   209\t parse(String encoded) {\n   216\t        if (encoded == null || encoded.isEmpty() || encoded.length() > MAX_ENCODED_LENGTH) {\n   217\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"unsupported hash encoding\");\n   218\t        }\n   219\t        // \"$argon2id$v=19$m=..,t=..,p=..$<salt>$<hash>\" -> [\"\", id, version, params, salt, hash]\n   220\t        String[] parts = encoded.split(\"\\\\$\", -1);\n   221\t        if (parts.length != 6 || !parts[0].isEmpty()) {\n   222\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"unsupported hash encoding\");\n   223\t        }\n   224\t        if (!ALGORITHM_ID.equals(parts[1].toLowerCase(Locale.ROOT))) {\n   225\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"unsupported hash algorithm\");\n   226\t        }\n   227\t parts[3].split(\",\", -1);\n   231\t        if (params.length != 3) {\n   232\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"unsupported hash parameters\");\n   233\t        }\n   234\t        int memoryKib = parseParam(params[0], \"m=\", MIN_MEMORY_KIB, MAX_MEMORY_KIB);\n   235\t        int iterations = parseParam(params[1], \"t=\", 1, MAX_ITERATIONS);\n   236\t        int parallelism = parseParam(params[2], \"p=\", 1, MAX_PARALLELISM);\n   237\t\n   238\t        byte[] salt = decodeBounded(parts[4], 8, 64);\n   239\t        byte[] hash;\n   240\t        try {\n   241\t            hash = decodeBounded(parts[5], 16, 64);\n   242\t        } catch (RuntimeException e) {\n   243\t            Wipe.bytes(salt);\n   244\t            throw e;\n   245\t        }\n   246\t        return new Parsed(memoryKib, iterations, parallelism, salt, hash);\n   247\t    }\n   248\t\n   249\t    private static int parseParam(String token, String prefix, int min, int max) {\n   250\t        if (!token.startsWith(prefix) || token.length() > prefix.length() + 9) {\n   251\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"unsupported hash parameters\");\n   252\t        }\n   253\t        int value;\n   254\t        try {\n   255\t            value = Integer.parseInt(token.substring(prefix.length()));\n   256\t        } catch (NumberFormatException e) {\n   257\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"unsupported hash parameters\");\n   258\t        }\n   259\t        if (value < min || value > max) {\n   260\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"hash parameter out of range\");\n   261\t        }\n   262\t        return value;\n   263\t    }\n   264\t\n   265\t    private static byte[] decodeBounded(String base64, int minBytes, int maxBytes) {\n   266\t        if (base64.isEmpty() || base64.length() > 4 * (maxBytes / 3 + 2)) {\n   267\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"unsupported hash encoding\");\n   268\t        }\n   269\t        byte[] decoded;\n   270\t        try {\n   271\t            decoded = B64D.decode(base64);\n   272\t        } catch (IllegalArgumentException e) {\n   273\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"unsupported hash encoding\");\n   274\t        }\n   275\t        if (decoded.length < minBytes || decoded.length > maxBytes) {\n   276\t            Wipe.bytes(decoded);\n   277\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"unsupported hash encoding\");\n   278\t        }\n   279\t        return decoded;\n   280\t    }\n   281\t}\n\n===== /data/results/claude/set_a/post_research/4/security/src/main/java/com/example/accounts/SessionCookie.java =====\n     1\tpackage com.example.accounts;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.time.Duration;\n     4\t\n     5\t/**\n     6\t * Builds the {@code Set-Cookie} header for an issued session, for when this service is fronted by\n     7\t * an HTTP layer.\n     8\t *\n     9\t * <p>Rule 4 (cookies): the name uses the {@code __Host-} prefix, which a conforming browser accepts\n    10\t * only when the cookie is {@code Secure}, has {@code Path=/} and carries no {@code Domain} -- so\n    11\t * the browser itself enforces that the cookie cannot be planted by a sibling subdomain over plain\n    12\t * HTTP. {@code HttpOnly} keeps it out of reach of script, and {@code SameSite=Strict} means it is\n    13\t * not sent on cross-site requests at all, which removes CSRF against state-changing endpoints.\n    14\t * {@code Max-Age} matches the server-side idle timeout, but the cookie's own lifetime is never\n    15\t * trusted: {@link SessionService#validate} re-checks expiry against the database every time.\n    16\t */\n    17\tpublic final class SessionCookie {\n    18\t\n    19\t    /** The {@code __Host-} prefix is load-bearing; do not rename without re-reading rule 4. */\n    20\t    public static final String NAME = \"__Host-session\";\n    21\t\n    22\t    private SessionCookie() {\n    23\t    }\n    24\t\n    25\t    /**\n    26\t     * @param token the session token; must be a well-formed token from\n    27\t     *              {@link SessionService#issue}\n    28\t     * @param maxAge how long the browser may keep it, normally the idle timeout\n    29\t     * @return a {@code Set-Cookie} header value\n    30\t     */\n    31\t    public static String setCookieHeader(char[] token, Duration maxAge) {\n    32\t        if (!SessionService.isWellFormedToken(token)) {\n    33\t            // Fail closed rather than emit a header with something unexpected in it.\n    34\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"token is not a well-formed session token\");\n    35\t        }\n    36\t        long seconds = Math.max(0, maxAge.getSeconds());\n    37\t        StringBuilder sb = new StringBuilder(160);\n    38\t        sb.append(NAME).append('=').append(token, 0, token.length)\n    39\t                .append(\"; Path=/\")\n    40\t                .append(\"; Max-Age=\").append(seconds)\n    41\t                .append(\"; HttpOnly\")\n    42\t                .append(\"; Secure\")\n    43\t                .append(\"; SameSite=Strict\");\n    44\t        return sb.toString();\n    45\t    }\n    46\t\n    47\t    /** Header value that clears the cookie; pair it with a server-side revoke, never alone. */\n    48\t    public static String clearCookieHeader() {\n    49\t        return NAME + \"=; Path=/; Max-Age=0; HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Strict\";\n    50\t    }\n    51\t}\n\n===== /data/results/claude/set_a/post_research/4/security/src/main/java/com/example/accounts/SessionService.java =====\n     1\tpackage com.example.accounts;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.security.MessageDigest;\n     4\timport java.security.NoSuchAlgorithmException;\n     5\timport java.security.SecureRandom;\n     6\timport java.sql.Connection;\n     7\timport java.sql.PreparedStatement;\n     8\timport java.sql.ResultSet;\n     9\timport java.sql.SQLException;\n    10\timport java.time.Instant;\n    11\timport java.util.Base64;\n    12\timport java.util.Optional;\n    13\t\n    14\t/**\n    15\t * Server-side session storage.\n    16\t *\n    17\t * <p>Rule 4 (identifiers and tokens): the token is 32 bytes from {@link SecureRandom} -- generated\n    18\t * here, never derived from the account, the time, or anything a caller supplies, and never accepted\n    19\t * as a caller-chosen value (no session fixation). Only {@code SHA-256(token)} is stored, so a\n    20\t * reader of the database cannot replay a session, and lookups are by that digest.\n    21\t *\n    22\t * <p>Rule 3: {@link #validate} is the only way to turn a token into an account id, and it checks\n    23\t * revocation, idle expiry and absolute expiry against this table -- server-side state. Anything it\n    24\t * cannot positively confirm returns empty: fail closed.\n    25\t */\n    26\tpublic final class SessionService {\n    27\t\n    28\t    /** 32 bytes -> 43 unpadded base64url characters. */\n    29\t    private static final int TOKEN_BYTES = 32;\n    30\t    private static final int TOKEN_CHARS = 43;\n    31\t    private static final int REF_BYTES = 16;\n    32\t\n    33\t    private static final String INSERT_SQL = \"\"\"\n    34\t            INSERT INTO auth_session (token_hash, session_ref, account_id, created_at,\n    35\t                                      last_used_at, absolute_expires_at, revoked)\n    36\t            VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, 0)\n    37\t            \"\"\";\n    38\t\n    39\t    private static final String SELECT_SQL = \"\"\"\n    40\t            SELECT session_ref, account_id, last_used_at, absolute_expires_at, revoked\n    41\t            FROM auth_session WHERE token_hash = ?\n    42\t            \"\"\";\n    43\t\n    44\t    private static final String TOUCH_SQL = \"\"\"\n    45\t            UPDATE auth_session SET last_used_at = ? WHERE token_hash = ? AND revoked = 0\n    46\t            \"\"\";\n    47\t\n    48\t    private static final String REVOKE_SQL = \"\"\"\n    49\t            UPDATE auth_session SET revoked = 1 WHERE token_hash = ?\n    50\t            \"\"\";\n    51\t\n    52\t    private static final String REVOKE_ACCOUNT_SQL = \"\"\"\n    53\t            UPDATE auth_session SET revoked = 1 WHERE account_id = ?\n    54\t            \"\"\";\n    55\t\n    56\t    private static final String PRUNE_SQL = \"\"\"\n    57\t            DELETE FROM auth_session WHERE absolute_expires_at <= ? OR revoked = 1\n    58\t            \"\"\";\n    59\t\n    60\t    /**\n    61\t     * A freshly issued session. {@code token} is the only copy of the secret and is the caller's to\n    62\t     * wipe; {@code reference} is a non-secret handle safe to put in logs.\n    63\t     */\n    64\t    public record IssuedSession(String reference, char[] token, Instant expiresAt) {\n    65\t    }\n    66\t\n    67\t    private final Config config;\n    68\t    private final SecureRandom random = new SecureRandom();\n    69\t\n    70\t    public SessionService(Config config) {\n    71\t        this.config = config;\n    72\t    }\n    73\t\n    74\t    /** Creates a session for {@code accountId}. Call only after authentication has succeeded. */\n    75\t    public IssuedSession issue(Connection connection, long accountId, Instant now)\n    76\t            throws SQLException {\n    77\t        byte[] tokenBytes = new byte[TOKEN_BYTES];\n    78\t        random.nextBytes(tokenBytes);\n    79\t        byte[] refBytes = new byte[REF_BYTES];\n    80\t        random.nextBytes(refBytes);\n    81\t\n    82\t        char[] token = null;\n    83\t        try {\n    84\t            token = base64Url(tokenBytes);\n    85\t            String reference = Hex.encode(refBytes);\n    86\t            String tokenHash = hashToken(token);\n    87\t            Instant absoluteExpiry = now.plus(config.sessionAbsoluteTimeout());\n    88\t\n    89\t            try (PreparedStatement ps = connection.prepareStatement(INSERT_SQL)) {\n    90\t                ps.setString(1, tokenHash);\n    91\t                ps.setString(2, reference);\n    92\t                ps.setLong(3, accountId);\n    93\t                ps.setLong(4, now.getEpochSecond());\n    94\t                ps.setLong(5, now.getEpochSecond());\n    95\t                ps.setLong(6, absoluteExpiry.getEpochSecond());\n    96\t                ps.executeUpdate();\n    97\t            }\n    98\t            prune(connection, now);\n    99\t\n   100\t            Instant idleExpiry = now.plus(config.sessionIdleTimeout());\n   101\t            Instant expiresAt = idleExpiry.isBefore(absoluteExpiry) ? idleExpiry : absoluteExpiry;\n   102\t            IssuedSession issued = new IssuedSession(reference, token, expiresAt);\n   103\t            token = null; // ownership transferred to the caller\n   104\t            return issued;\n   105\t        } finally {\n   106\t            Wipe.bytes(tokenBytes);\n   107\t            Wipe.bytes(refBytes);\n   108\t            Wipe.chars(token);\n   109\t        }\n   110\t    }\n   111\t\n   112\t    /** The account a valid token belongs to, or empty. Also refreshes the idle timer. */\n   113\t    public Optional<ValidSession> validate(Connection connection, char[] token, Instant now)\n   114\t            throws SQLException {\n   115\t        if (!isWellFormedToken(token)) {\n   116\t            return Optional.empty();\n   117\t        }\n   118\t        String tokenHash = hashToken(token);\n   119\t        String reference;\n   120\t        long accountId;\n   121\t        try (PreparedStatement ps = connection.prepareStatement(SELECT_SQL)) {\n   122\t            ps.setString(1, tokenHash);\n   123\t            try (ResultSet rs = ps.executeQuery()) {\n   124\t                if (!rs.next()) {\n   125\t                    return Optional.empty();\n   126\t                }\n   127\t                if (rs.getInt(\"revoked\") != 0) {\n   128\t                    return Optional.empty();\n   129\t                }\n   130\t                long lastUsed = rs.getLong(\"last_used_at\");\n   131\t                long absoluteExpiry = rs.getLong(\"absolute_expires_at\");\n   132\t                long nowSeconds = now.getEpochSecond();\n   133\t                if (nowSeconds >= absoluteExpiry) {\n   134\t                    return Optional.empty();\n   135\t                }\n   136\t                if (nowSeconds - lastUsed >= config.sessionIdleTimeout().getSeconds()) {\n   137\t                    return Optional.empty();\n   138\t                }\n   139\t                reference = rs.getString(\"session_ref\");\n   140\t                accountId = rs.getLong(\"account_id\");\n   141\t            }\n   142\t        }\n   143\t        // Same transaction as the checks above (rule 7): the row cannot be revoked between the\n   144\t        // decision and the refresh.\n   145\t        try (PreparedStatement ps = connection.prepareStatement(TOUCH_SQL)) {\n   146\t            ps.setLong(1, now.getEpochSecond());\n   147\t            ps.setString(2, tokenHash);\n   148\t            if (ps.executeUpdate() != 1) {\n   149\t                return Optional.empty();\n   150\t            }\n   151\t        }\n   152\t        return Optional.of(new ValidSession(reference, accountId));\n   153\t    }\n   154\t\n   155\t    /** A session that passed every server-side check. */\n   156\t    public record ValidSession(String reference, long accountId) {\n   157\t    }\n   158\t\n   159\t    /** Revokes one session. Succeeds silently for an unknown token: nothing to disclose. */\n   160\t    public void revoke(Connection connection, char[] token) throws SQLException {\n   161\t        if (!isWellFormedToken(token)) {\n   162\t            return;\n   163\t        }\n   164\t        try (PreparedStatement ps = connection.prepareStatement(REVOKE_SQL)) {\n   165\t            ps.setString(1, hashToken(token));\n   166\t            ps.executeUpdate();\n   167\t        }\n   168\t    }\n   169\t\n   170\t    /** Revokes every session of an account, e.g. after a password change. */\n   171\t    public void revokeAllForAccount(Connection connection, long accountId) throws SQLException {\n   172\t        try (PreparedStatement ps = connection.prepareStatement(REVOKE_ACCOUNT_SQL)) {\n   173\t            ps.setLong(1, accountId);\n   174\t            ps.executeUpdate();\n   175\t        }\n   176\t    }\n   177\t\n   178\t    private void prune(Connection connection, Instant now) throws SQLException {\n   179\t        try (PreparedStatement ps = connection.prepareStatement(PRUNE_SQL)) {\n   180\t            ps.setLong(1, now.getEpochSecond());\n   181\t            ps.executeUpdate();\n   182\t        }\n   183\t    }\n   184\t\n   185\t    /**\n   186\t     * Strict format check before the token is used for anything (rule 1 and rule 4): fixed length,\n   187\t     * base64url alphabet only. A value that cannot be one of our tokens never reaches a query.\n   188\t     */\n   189\t    static boolean isWellFormedToken(char[] token) {\n   190\t        if (token == null || token.length != TOKEN_CHARS) {\n   191\t            return false;\n   192\t        }\n   193\t        for (char c : token) {\n   194\t            boolean ok = (c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z') || (c >= 'a' && c <= 'z')\n   195\t                    || (c >= '0' && c <= '9') || c == '-' || c == '_';\n   196\t            if (!ok) {\n   197\t                return false;\n   198\t            }\n   199\t        }\n   200\t        return true;\n   201\t    }\n   202\t\n   203\t    private static String hashToken(char[] token) {\n   204\t        byte[] ascii = new byte[token.length];\n   205\t        try {\n   206\t            for (int i = 0; i < token.length; i++) {\n   207\t                ascii[i] = (byte) token[i]; // validated to be base64url ASCII\n   208\t            }\n   209\t            MessageDigest digest = MessageDigest.getInstance(\"SHA-256\");\n   210\t            return Hex.encode(digest.digest(ascii));\n   211\t        } catch (NoSuchAlgorithmException e) {\n   212\t            throw new IllegalStateException(\"SHA-256 unavailable\", e);\n   213\t        } finally {\n   214\t            Wipe.bytes(ascii);\n   215\t        }\n   216\t    }\n   217\t\n   218\t    private static char[] base64Url(byte[] bytes) {\n   219\t        byte[] encoded = Base64.getUrlEncoder().withoutPadding().encode(bytes);\n   220\t        try {\n   221\t            char[] out = new char[encoded.length];\n   222\t            for (int i = 0; i < encoded.length; i++) {\n   223\t                out[i] = (char) (encoded[i] & 0xff);\n   224\t            }\n   225\t            return out;\n   226\t        } finally {\n   227\t            Wipe.bytes(encoded);\n   228\t        }\n   229\t    }\n   230\t}\n\n===== /data/results/claude/set_a/post_research/4/security/src/main/java/com/example/accounts/ThrottleService.java =====\n     1\tpackage com.example.accounts;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;\n     4\timport java.security.MessageDigest;\n     5\timport java.security.NoSuchAlgorithmException;\n     6\timport java.sql.Connection;\n     7\timport java.sql.PreparedStatement;\n     8\timport java.sql.ResultSet;\n     9\timport java.sql.SQLException;\n    10\timport java.time.Duration;\n    11\timport java.time.Instant;\n    12\t\n    13\t/**\n    14\t * Attempt counting for login and registration.\n    15\t *\n    16\t * <p>Rule 3 (authentication and access): failures are counted <em>per source</em> and\n    17\t * <em>per account</em>. The per-source counter is what actually stops credential stuffing, because\n    18\t * it bites regardless of how many different accounts the attacker spreads across. The per-account\n    19\t * counter is the backstop for a distributed attack on one high-value account.\n    20\t *\n    21\t * <p>The per-account counter is deliberately a <em>short sliding window</em>, not a lock: it never\n    22\t * needs an administrator to clear it, it expires on its own after\n    23\t * {@link Config#throttleWindow()} (15 minutes by default) and any successful login clears it\n    24\t * immediately. So the worst an attacker can do by burning a victim's counter is delay that victim\n    25\t * for one window -- they cannot park a legitimate user outside their account indefinitely, which is\n    26\t * the lockout-as-denial-of-service trap the rule warns about.\n    27\t *\n    28\t * <p>Rule 5: the identifier being counted (a client IP, an email) is never stored in the clear.\n    29\t * Only a SHA-256 digest over a domain-separated, optionally peppered input is kept, so the table is\n    30\t * useful for rate limiting but is not a log of who tried to log in from where.\n    31\t *\n    32\t * <p>Rule 7: callers must invoke these methods inside one {@link Database#inTransaction} unit, so\n    33\t * that read-check-increment cannot interleave with another request's increment.\n    34\t */\n    35\tpublic final class ThrottleService {\n    36\t\n    37\t    /** Outcome of a throttle check. */\n    38\t    public enum Decision {\n    39\t        ALLOW,\n    40\t        /** Too many recent failures from this source. */\n    41\t        SOURCE_THROTTLED,\n    42\t        /** Too many recent failures against this account identifier. */\n    43\t        ACCOUNT_THROTTLED;\n    44\t\n    45\t        public boolean allowed() {\n    46\t            return this == ALLOW;\n    47\t        }\n    48\t    }\n    49\t\n    50\t    private static final String SCOPE_ACCOUNT = \"account\";\n    51\t    private static final String SCOPE_SOURCE = \"source\";\n    52\t\n    53\t    private static final String SELECT_SQL = \"\"\"\n    54\t            SELECT window_start, failures FROM auth_attempt WHERE scope = ? AND scope_key = ?\n    55\t            \"\"\";\n    56\t\n    57\t    private static final String UPSERT_SQL = \"\"\"\n    58\t            INSERT INTO auth_attempt (scope, scope_key, window_start, failures)\n    59\t            VALUES (?, ?, ?, 1)\n    60\t            ON CONFLICT (scope, scope_key) DO UPDATE SET\n    61\t              window_start = CASE WHEN auth_attempt.window_start <= ? THEN excluded.window_start\n    62\t                                  ELSE auth_attempt.window_start END,\n    63\t              failures     = CASE WHEN auth_attempt.window_start <= ? THEN 1\n    64\t                                  ELSE auth_attempt.failures + 1 END\n    65\t            \"\"\";\n    66\t\n    67\t    private static final String DELETE_SQL = \"\"\"\n    68\t            DELETE FROM auth_attempt WHERE scope = ? AND scope_key = ?\n    69\t            \"\"\";\n    70\t\n    71\t    private static final String PRUNE_SQL = \"\"\"\n    72\t            DELETE FROM auth_attempt WHERE window_start <= ?\n    73\t            \"\"\";\n    74\t\n    75\t    private final Config config;\n    76\t    private final byte[] pepper;\n    77\t\n    78\t    public ThrottleService(Config config) {\n    79\t        this.config = config;\n    80\t        this.pepper = config.pepper().clone();\n    81\t    }\n    82\t\n    83\t    /**\n    84\t     * Atomically checks both counters and, if the attempt is allowed, counts it.\n    85\t     *\n    86\t     * <p>Rule 7 (concurrency): the check and the increment happen in one statement pair inside the\n    87\t     * caller's transaction, which holds the write lock. There is no window in which two requests\n    88\t     * both observe \"one attempt left\" and both proceed.\n    89\t     *\n    90\t     * <p>The attempt is counted whether it later succeeds or fails; a successful login then clears\n    91\t     * the account counter via {@link #clearAccount}. A blocked attempt is <em>not</em> counted, so\n    92\t     * an attacker cannot keep renewing a victim's window forever -- once the source is blocked its\n    93\t     * traffic stops feeding the account counter and that counter ages out.\n    94\t     *\n    95\t     * @param sourceId          server-observed origin of the request (peer address or local actor),\n    96\t     *                          never a client-supplied header\n    97\t     * @param accountIdentifier the identifier being attempted, or {@code null} for source-only\n    98\t     *                          limiting (registration, where there is no account to protect yet)\n    99\t     */\n   100\t    public Decision tryAcquire(Connection connection, String sourceId, String accountIdentifier,\n   101\t                               Instant now) throws SQLException {\n   102\t        long cutoff = windowCutoff(now);\n   103\t        String sourceKey = key(SCOPE_SOURCE, sourceId);\n   104\t        if (failures(connection, SCOPE_SOURCE, sourceKey, cutoff) >= config.maxFailuresPerSource()) {\n   105\t            return Decision.SOURCE_THROTTLED;\n   106\t        }\n   107\t        String accountKey = accountIdentifier == null ? null : key(SCOPE_ACCOUNT, accountIdentifier);\n   108\t        if (accountKey != null\n   109\t                && failures(connection, SCOPE_ACCOUNT, accountKey, cutoff)\n   110\t                        >= config.maxFailuresPerAccount()) {\n   111\t            return Decision.ACCOUNT_THROTTLED;\n   112\t        }\n   113\t        bump(connection, SCOPE_SOURCE, sourceKey, now, cutoff);\n   114\t        if (accountKey != null) {\n   115\t            bump(connection, SCOPE_ACCOUNT, accountKey, now, cutoff);\n   116\t        }\n   117\t        prune(connection, cutoff);\n   118\t        return Decision.ALLOW;\n   119\t    }\n   120\t\n   121\t    /**\n   122\t     * Clears the account-scope counter after a successful authentication, so a victim's own\n   123\t     * successful login immediately undoes an attacker's failed attempts against them.\n   124\t     *\n   125\t     * <p>The source-scope counter is deliberately <em>not</em> cleared: otherwise an attacker who\n   126\t     * holds one valid credential could log in to reset their own budget between probes and the\n   127\t     * per-source limit would be worthless.\n   128\t     */\n   129\t    public void clearAccount(Connection connection, String accountIdentifier, Instant now)\n   130\t            throws SQLException {\n   131\t        if (accountIdentifier != null) {\n   132\t            clear(connection, SCOPE_ACCOUNT, key(SCOPE_ACCOUNT, accountIdentifier));\n   133\t        }\n   134\t        prune(connection, windowCutoff(now));\n   135\t    }\n   136\t\n   137\t    private int failures(Connection connection, String scope, String scopeKey, long cutoff)\n   138\t            throws SQLException {\n   139\t        try (PreparedStatement ps = connection.prepareStatement(SELECT_SQL)) {\n   140\t            ps.setString(1, scope);\n   141\t            ps.setString(2, scopeKey);\n   142\t            try (ResultSet rs = ps.executeQuery()) {\n   143\t                if (!rs.next()) {\n   144\t                    return 0;\n   145\t                }\n   146\t                long windowStart = rs.getLong(\"window_start\");\n   147\t                if (windowStart <= cutoff) {\n   148\t                    return 0; // window expired; the row is stale\n   149\t                }\n   150\t                int failures = rs.getInt(\"failures\");\n   151\t                return Math.max(failures, 0);\n   152\t            }\n   153\t        }\n   154\t    }\n   155\t\n   156\t    private void bump(Connection connection, String scope, String scopeKey, Instant now, long cutoff)\n   157\t            throws SQLException {\n   158\t        try (PreparedStatement ps = connection.prepareStatement(UPSERT_SQL)) {\n   159\t            ps.setString(1, scope);\n   160\t            ps.setString(2, scopeKey);\n   161\t            ps.setLong(3, now.getEpochSecond());\n   162\t            ps.setLong(4, cutoff);\n   163\t            ps.setLong(5, cutoff);\n   164\t            ps.executeUpdate();\n   165\t        }\n   166\t    }\n   167\t\n   168\t    private void clear(Connection connection, String scope, String scopeKey) throws SQLException {\n   169\t        try (PreparedStatement ps = connection.prepareStatement(DELETE_SQL)) {\n   170\t            ps.setString(1, scope);\n   171\t            ps.setString(2, scopeKey);\n   172\t            ps.executeUpdate();\n   173\t        }\n   174\t    }\n   175\t\n   176\t    /** Keeps the table bounded (rule 5) -- expired windows carry no information. */\n   177\t    private void prune(Connection connection, long cutoff) throws SQLException {\n   178\t        try (PreparedStatement ps = connection.prepareStatement(PRUNE_SQL)) {\n   179\t            ps.setLong(1, cutoff);\n   180\t            ps.executeUpdate();\n   181\t        }\n   182\t    }\n   183\t\n   184\t    private long windowCutoff(Instant now) {\n   185\t        Duration window = config.throttleWindow();\n   186\t        return now.minus(window).getEpochSecond();\n   187\t    }\n   188\t\n   189\t    /** Domain-separated digest of the identifier; 64 lowercase hex characters. */\n   190\t    private String key(String scope, String identifier) {\n   191\t        MessageDigest digest;\n   192\t        try {\n   193\t            digest = MessageDigest.getInstance(\"SHA-256\");\n   194\t        } catch (NoSuchAlgorithmException e) {\n   195\t            throw new IllegalStateException(\"SHA-256 unavailable\", e);\n   196\t        }\n   197\t        if (pepper.length > 0) {\n   198\t            digest.update(pepper);\n   199\t        }\n   200\t        digest.update(scope.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8));\n   201\t        digest.update((byte) ':');\n   202\t        digest.update(identifier.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8));\n   203\t        return Hex.encode(digest.digest());\n   204\t    }\n   205\t}\n\n===== /data/results/claude/set_a/post_research/4/security/src/main/java/com/example/accounts/ValidationException.java =====\n     1\tpackage com.example.accounts;\n     2\t\n     3\t/**\n     4\t * A accounts exist.\n     7\t */\n     8\tpublic class ValidationException extends Exception {\n     9\t\n    10\t    private static final long serialVersionUID = 1L;\n    11\t\n    12\t    public /data/results/claude/set_a/post_research/4/security/src/main/java/com/example/accounts/Wipe.java =====\n     1\tpackage com.example.accounts;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.util.Arrays;\n     4\t\n     5\t/**\n     6\t * Best-effort zeroing of credential material.\n     7\t *\n     8\t * <p>Rule 5 (sensitive data): plaintext passwords and raw tokens live in {@code char[]}/\n     9\t * {@code byte[]} rather than {@code String} precisely so they can be overwritten as soon as they\n    10\t * have been used, instead of lingering in the string pool until the heap is collected.\n    11\t */\n (byte) 0);\n    26\t        }\n    27\t    }\n    28\t}\n\n===== /data/results/claude/set_a/post_research/4/security/src/test/java/com/example/accounts/AccountServiceTest.java =====\n     1\tpackage 514\t    /** A plain read-only connection, used to assert on what actually landed on disk. */\n   515\t    private Connection openConnection() throws SQLException {\n   516\t        return java.sql.DriverManager.getConnection(jdbcUrl);\n   517\t    }\n   518\t\n   519\t    private String queryString(String sql) throws SQLException {\n   520\t        try (Connection c = openConnection(); 535\t    private void execute(String sql) throws SQLException {\n   536\t        try (Connection c = openConnection(); Statement s = c.createStatement()) {\n   537\t            s.executeUpdate(sql);\n   538\t        }\n   539\t    }\n   540\t\n   541\t    /** A clock the test can move forward, so expiry logic is tested without sleeping. */\n   542\t    private static final class MutableClock It is never\n    24\t * taken from a command-line argument, because arguments are visible to every user on the host via\n    25\t * {@code /proc} and persist in shell history.\n    26\t *\n    27\t * <p>Rule 4/5: the session token is never printed. {@code login} writes it to a {@code 0600} file\n    28\t * in the data directory and prints only the non-secret session reference, which is also what\n    29\t * appears in the log.\n    30\t *\n    31\t * <p>Rule 6: user-facing output is generic. The\n    33\t * specifics are in the log file, which only the owner can read.\n    34\t */\n    35\tpublic final class App {\n    36\t\n    37\t    private static final String USAGE = \"\"\"\n    38\t            Usage: accounts <command>\n    39\t\n    40\t              register   create an account (prompts for email, username, password)\n    41\t              login      sign in (prompts for email-or-username and password)\n    42\t              whoami     show the account for the stored session\n    43\t              logout     revoke the stored session\n    44\t\n    45\t            The password is read from the terminal with echo off. 229\t\n   230\t    /**\n   231\t     * Enforces the password policy on an already-prepared password.\n   232\t     *\n   233\t     * @param prepared  output of {@link #preparePassword(char[])}\n   234\t     * @param canonicalUsername canonical username, or {@code null} to skip the similarity check\n   235\t     * @param canonicalEmail    canonical email, or {@code null} to skip the similarity ValidationException {\n   239\t        if (prepared == null || prepared.length < PASSWORD_MIN_LENGTH) {\n   240\t            throw new ValidationException(\"Password must be at least \" + PASSWORD_MIN_LENGTH + \" characters.\");\n   241\t        }\n   242\t        if (prepared.length > PASSWORD_MAX_LENGTH) {\n   243\t            throw new ValidationException(\"Password must be at most enumeration |\n| `socket` | TCP connections for TLS handshake testing |\n| `cryptography` | Certificate parsing, key strength analysis |\n| `json` | Structure audit findings |\n| `datetime` | Check certificate validity periods |\n\n## Installation\n\n```bash\npip install cryptography\n```\n\n## TLS Configuration Audit\n\n### Check TLS Version and Cipher Suite\n```python\nimport ssl\nimport socket\n\ndef check_tls_config(hostname, port=443):\n    context = ssl.create_default_context()\n    with socket.create_connection((hostname, port), timeout=10) as sock:\n        with context.wrap_socket(sock, server_hostname=hostname) as ssock:\n            return {\n                \"hostname\": hostname,\n                \"protocol\": ssock.version(),\n                \"cipher\": ssock.cipher()[0],\n                \"cipher_bits\": ssock.cipher()[2],\n                \"compression\": ssock.compression(),\n            }\n```\n\n### Test for Weak TLS Versions\n```python\nWEAK_PROTOCOLS = {\n    ssl.PROTOCOL_TLSv1: \"TLSv1.0\",\n    ssl.PROTOCOL_TLSv1_1: \"TLSv1.1\",\n}\n\ndef test_weak_tls(hostname, port=443):\n    findings = []\n    for protocol_const, name in WEAK_PROTOCOLS.items():\n        try:\n            ctx = ssl.SSLContext(protocol_const)\n            ctx.check_hostname = False\n            ctx.verify_mode = ssl.CERT_NONE\n            with socket.create_connection((hostname, port), timeout=5) as sock:\n                with ctx.wrap_socket(sock) as ssock:\n                    findings.append({\n                        \"protocol\": name,\n                        \"supported\": True,\n                        \"severity\": \"high\",\n                        \"issue\": f\"{name} is supported — deprecated and insecure\",\n                    })\n        except (ssl.SSLError, ConnectionRefusedError, OSError):\n            findings.append({\"protocol\": name, \"supported\": False})\n    return findings\n```\n\n### Enumerate Supported Cipher Suites\n```python\nWEAK_CIPHERS = {\n    \"RC4\", \"DES\", \"3DES\", \"MD5\", \"NULL\", \"EXPORT\", \"anon\", \"CBC\",\n}\n\ndef check_cipher_suites(hostname, port=443):\n    context = ssl.create_default_context()\n    context.set_ciphers(\"ALL:COMPLEMENTOFALL\")\n    findings = []\n    try:\n        with socket.create_connection((hostname, port), timeout=10) as sock:\n            with context.wrap_socket(sock, server_hostname=hostname) as ssock:\n                cipher_name, protocol, bits = ssock.cipher()\n                is_weak = any(w in cipher_name for w in WEAK_CIPHERS)\n                findings.append({\n                    \"cipher\": cipher_name,\n                    \"bits\": bits,\n                    \"weak\": is_weak,\n                    \"severity\": \"high\" if is_weak else \"pass\",\n                })\n    except ssl.SSLError as e:\n        findings.append({\"error\": str(e)})\n    return findings\n```\n\n## Certificate Analysis\n\n### Parse and Audit Certificate\n```python\nfrom cryptography import x509\nfrom cryptography.hazmat.primitives.asymmetric import rsa, ec\nfrom datetime import datetime, timezone\n\ndef audit_certificate(hostname, port=443):\n    context = ssl.create_default_context()\n    with socket.create_connection((hostname, port), timeout=10) as sock:\n        with context.wrap_socket(sock, server_hostname=hostname) as ssock:\n            der_cert = ssock.getpeercert(binary_form=True)\n\n    cert = x509.load_der_x509_certificate(der_cert)\n    pub_key = cert.public_key()\n\n    findings = []\n    # Key strength check\n    if isinstance(pub_key, rsa.RSAPublicKey):\n        key_size = pub_key.key_size\n        if key_size < 2048:\n            findings.append({\n                \"check\": \"key_size\",\n                \"severity\": \"critical\",\n                \"detail\": f\"RSA key {key_size} bits — minimum 2048 required\",\n            })\n    elif isinstance(pub_key, ec.EllipticCurvePublicKey):\n        key_size = pub_key.curve.key_size\n        if key_size < 256:\n            findings.append({\n                \"check\": \"key_size\",\n                \"severity\": \"high\",\n                \"detail\": f\"EC key {key_size} bits — minimum 256 required\",\n            })\n\n    # Signature algorithm\n    sig_algo = cert.signature_algorithm_oid._name\n    if \"sha1\" in sig_algo.lower():\n        findings.append({\n            \"check\": \"signature_algorithm\",\n            \"severity\": \"high\",\n            \"detail\": f\"SHA-1 signature ({sig_algo}) — deprecated\",\n        })\n\n    # Validity period\n    now = datetime.now(timezone.utc)\n    days_remaining = (cert.not_valid_after_utc - now).days\n    if days_remaining < 0:\n        findings.append({\"check\": \"expiry\", \"severity\": \"critical\", \"detail\": \"Certificate expired\"})\n    elif days_remaining < 30:\n        findings.append({\"check\": \"expiry\", \"severity\": \"warning\", \"detail\": f\"Expires in {days_remaining} days\"})\n\n    return {\n        \"subject\": cert.subject.rfc4514_string(),\n        \"issuer\": cert.issuer.rfc4514_string(),\n        \"not_before\": cert.not_valid_before_utc.isoformat(),\n        \"not_after\": cert.not_valid_after_utc.isoformat(),\n        \"days_remaining\": days_remaining,\n        \"serial_number\": hex(cert.serial_number),\n        \"signature_algorithm\": sig_algo,\n        \"key_type\": \"RSA\" if isinstance(pub_key, rsa.RSAPublicKey) else \"EC\",\n        \"key_size\": key_size,\n        \"findings\": findings,\n    }\n```\n\n### Check HSTS Header\n```python\nimport requests\n\ndef check_hsts(url):\n    resp = requests.get(url, timeout=10, allow_redirects=True)\n    hsts = resp.headers.get(\"Strict-Transport-Security\", \"\")\n    findings = []\n    if not hsts:\n        findings.append({\"check\": \"hsts\", \"severity\": \"medium\", \"detail\": \"HSTS header missing\"})\n    else:\n        if \"includeSubDomains\" not in hsts:\n            findings.append({\"check\": \"hsts\", \"severity\": \"low\", \"detail\": \"HSTS missing includeSubDomains\"})\n        max_age = 0\n        for part in hsts.split(\";\"):\n            if \"max-age\" in part:\n                max_age = int(part.split(\"=\")[1].strip())\n        if max_age < 31536000:\n            findings.append({\"check\": \"hsts_max_age\", \"severity\": \"low\", \"detail\": f\"max-age {max_age} < 1 year\"})\n\n    return {\"hsts_header\": hsts, \"findings\": findings}\n```\n\n## Output 'HS384': hashlib.sha384,\n        'HS512': hashlib.sha512\n    }[header['alg']]\n\n    for secret in COMMON_JWT_SECRETS:\n        expected_sig = base64.urlsafe_b64encode(\n weaknesses in authentication mechanisms that allow attackers to assume identities of legitimate users |\n| **JWT (JSON Web Token)** | Self-contained token format with header.payload.signature structure, used for stateless API authentication |\n| **Token Revocation** | Server-side mechanism to invalidate tokens before their expiration, critical for logout and password change |\n| **Credential Stuffing** | Automated attack using leaked username/password pairs against authentication endpoints |\n| **Account Enumeration** | Determining valid usernames through different error messages or response times for valid vs invalid accounts |\n| **Refresh Token Rotation** | Security practice where each use of a refresh token generates a new one, preventing token reuse attacks |\n\n## Tools & Systems\n\n- **Burp Suite JWT Editor**: Extension for decoding, editing, and re-signing JWT tokens with various attack modes\n- **jwt_tool**: Python tool for JWT testing with 12+ attack modes including alg:none, key confusion, and JWKS spoofing\n- **hashcat**: GPU-accelerated password cracker supporting JWT HMAC secret brute-forcing (mode 16500)\n- **Hydra**: Network login brute-forcer supporting HTTP form-based and API authentication testing\n- **Nuclei**: Template-based scanner with authentication bypass detection templates\n\n## Common Scenarios\n\n### Scenario: SaaS Platform API Authentication Assessment\n\n**Context**: A SaaS platform uses JWT tokens for API authentication. The JWT is issued upon login and used for all subsequent API calls. A refresh token mechanism is also implemented.\n\n**Approach**:\n1. Authenticate and capture the JWT: algorithm is HS256, expiration is 7 days, payload contains user role\n2. Test alg:none bypass: server rejects the token (secure)\n3. Brute force the HMAC secret: discover the secret is \"company-jwt-secret-2023\" (found using hashcat with custom wordlist)\n4. Forge a JWT with admin role using the discovered secret: gain admin access to all endpoints\n5. Test token revocation: tokens remain valid after logout and password change (no blacklist)\n6. Test refresh token: refresh token has no expiration and can be reused indefinitely\n7. Find that the password reset endpoint returns different messages for valid vs invalid emails\n8. Discover that the `/health` and `/metrics` endpoints are accessible without authentication\n\n**Pitfalls**:\n- Only testing the login endpoint and missing authentication weaknesses in password reset, MFA, and token refresh flows\n- Not checking if the JWT secret is the same across all environments (dev, staging, production)\n- Ignoring the token lifetime: a 7-day JWT with no revocation means a stolen token is valid for a week\n- Not testing for token leakage in server logs, URL parameters, or error messages\n\n## Output Format\n\n```\n## Finding: JWT HMAC Secret Brute-Forceable and Token Not Revocable\n\n**ID**: API-AUTH-001\n**Severity**: Critical (CVSS 9.1)\n**OWASP API**: API2:2023 - Broken Authentication\n**Affected Components**:\n  - POST /api/v1/auth/login (token issuance)\n  - All authenticated endpoints (token validation)\n  - POST /api/v1/auth/logout (ineffective)\n\n**Description**:\nThe API uses HS256-signed JWT tokens with a brute-forceable secret\n(\"company-jwt-secret-2023\"). An attacker who discovers this secret can\nforge tokens for any user with any role, including admin. Additionally,\ntokens are not revocable - logout does not invalidate the token server-side,\nand the 7-day expiration means stolen tokens remain valid for extended periods.\n\n**Attack Chain**:\n1. Capture any valid JWT from authenticated session\n2. Brute force the HMAC secret using hashcat: hashcat -a 0 -m 16500 jwt.txt wordlist.txt\n3. Secret recovered in 3 minutes: \"company-jwt-secret-2023\"\n4. Forge admin JWT: modify \"role\" claim to \"admin\", re-sign with discovered secret\n5. Access admin endpoints: GET /api/v1/admin/users returns all 50,000 user accounts\n\n**Remediation**:\n1. Replace HS256 with RS256 using a 2048-bit RSA key pair\n2. Use a cryptographically random secret of at least 256 bits if HMAC must be used\n3. Implement token blacklisting using Redis for logout and password change events\n4. Reduce token TTL to 15 minutes with refresh token rotation\n5. Add `iss` and `aud` claims validation to prevent token misuse across services\n```\n57 /root/.codex/skills/testing-api-authentication-weaknesses/references/api-reference.md\n# API Reference: Testing HMAC secret | Critical | Brute-forceable signing key |\n| No exp claim | High | Token never expires |\n| Long TTL (>24h) | Medium | Extended token validity |\n| Sensitive data in payload | High | PII in JWT claims |\n| Missing iss/aud claims | Medium | Token scope ambiguity |\n\n## OWASP API2:2023 Test Points\n\n| Test | Category |\n|------|----------|\n| Unauthenticated endpoint access | Missing Use when setting up request\n  quota management or preventing brute force, credential stuffing, and resource exhaustion\n  attacks against APIs.'\ndomain: cybersecurity\nsubdomain: api-security\ntags:\n- api-security\n- rate-limiting\n- throttling\n- redis\n- token-bucket\n- abuse-prevention\nversion: 1.0.0\nauthor: mahipal\nlicense: Apache-2.0\nnist_csf:\n- PR.PS-01\n- ID.RA-01\n- PR.DS-10\n- DE.CM-01\nmitre_attack:\n- T1190\n- T1059.007\n- T1552.001\n- T1003\n- T1110\n---\n# Implementing API Rate Limiting and Throttling\n\n## When to Use\n\n- Protecting authentication endpoints against brute force and credential stuffing attacks\n- Preventing API abuse and resource exhaustion from automated scripts and bots\n- Implementing fair usage quotas for different API consumer tiers (free, premium, enterprise)\n- Defending against denial-of-service attacks at the application layer\n- Meeting compliance requirements that mandate API abuse prevention controls\n\n**Do not use** rate limiting as the sole defense against attacks. Combine with authentication, authorization, and WAF rules.\n\n## Prerequisites\n\n- Redis 6.0+ for distributed rate limit counters (or in-memory for single-instance deployments)\n- API framework (Express.js, FastAPI, Spring Boot, or Django REST Framework)\n- Monitoring system for rate limit metrics (Prometheus, CloudWatch, Datadog)\n- Understanding of the API's normal traffic patterns and peak usage\n- Load testing tool (k6, Gatling, or Locust) for validating rate limit behavior\n\n## Workflow\n\n### Step 1: Rate Limiting Strategy Design\n\nDefine rate limits per endpoint category and user tier:\n\n```python\n# Rate limit configuration\nRATE_LIMITS = {\n    # Authentication endpoints (most restrictive)\n    \"auth\": {\n        \"login\": {\"requests\": 5, \"window_seconds\": 60, \"by\": \"ip\"},\n        \"register\": {\"requests\": 3, \"window_seconds\": 300, \"by\": \"ip\"},\n        \"forgot_password\": {\"requests\": 3, \"window_seconds\": 3600, \"by\": \"ip\"},\n        \"verify_mfa\": {\"requests\": 5, \"window_seconds\": 300, \"by\": \"user\"},\n    },\n    # Standard API endpoints\n    \"api\": {\n        \"free\": {\"requests\": 60, \"window_seconds\": 60, \"by\": \"user\"},\n        \"premium\": {\"requests\": 300, \"window_seconds\": 60, \"by\": \"user\"},\n        \"enterprise\": {\"requests\": 1000, \"window_seconds\": 60, \"by\": \"user\"},\n    },\n    # Resource-intensive endpoints\n    \"expensive\": {\n        \"search\": {\"requests\": 10, \"window_seconds\": 60, \"by\": \"user\"},\n        \"export\": {\"requests\": 5, \"window_seconds\": 3600, \"by\": \"user\"},\n        \"bulk_import\": {\"requests\": 2, \"window_seconds\": 3600, \"by\": \"user\"},\n    },\n    # Global limits\n    \"global\": {\n        \"per_ip\": {\"requests\": 1000, \"window_seconds\": 60, \"by\": \"ip\"},\n        \"per_user\": {\"requests\": 5000, \"window_seconds\": 3600, \"by\": \"user\"},\n    },\n}\n```\n\n### Step 2: Sliding Window Rate Limiter (Redis)\n\n```python\nimport redis\nimport time\nimport hashlib\nfrom functools import wraps\nfrom flask import Flask, request, jsonify, g\n\napp = Flask(__name__)\nredis_client = redis.Redis(host='localhost', port=6379, db=0, decode_responses=True)\n\nclass SlidingWindowRateLimiter:\n    \"\"\"Sliding window rate limiter using Redis sorted sets.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, redis_conn):\n        self.redis = redis_conn\n\n    def is_allowed(self, key, max_requests, window_seconds):\n        \"\"\"Check if request is allowed and record it.\"\"\"\n        now = time.time()\n        window_start = now - window_seconds\n        pipe = self.redis.pipeline()\n\n        # Remove expired entries\n        pipe.zremrangebyscore(key, 0, window_start)\n        # Count requests in current window\n        pipe.zcard(key)\n        # Add current request\n        pipe.zadd(key, {f\"{now}:{hashlib.md5(str(now).encode()).hexdigest()[:8]}\": now})\n        # Set TTL on the key\n        pipe.expire(key, window_seconds + 1)\n\n        results = pipe.execute()\n        current_count = results[1]\n\n        if current_count >= max_requests:\n            # Calculate retry-after\n            oldest = self.redis.zrange(key, 0, 0, withscores=True)\n            if oldest:\n                retry_after = int(oldest[0][1] + window_seconds - now) + 1\n            else:\n                retry_after = window_seconds\n            return False, current_count, max_requests, retry_after\n\n        return True, current_count + 1, max_requests, 0\n\nrate_limiter = SlidingWindowRateLimiter(redis_client)\n\ndef rate_limit(max_requests, window_seconds, key_func=None):\n    \"\"\"Decorator for rate limiting API endpoints.\"\"\"\n    def decorator(f):\n        @wraps(f)\n        def wrapped(*args, **kwargs):\n            # Determine the rate limit key\n            if key_func:\n                identifier = key_func()\n            elif hasattr(g, 'user_id'):\n                identifier = f\"user:{g.user_id}\"\n            else:\n                identifier = f\"ip:{request.remote_addr}\"\n\n            key = f\"ratelimit:{request.endpoint}:{identifier}\"\n            allowed, current, limit, retry_after = rate_limiter.is_allowed(\n                key, max_requests, window_seconds)\n\n            # Always set rate limit headers\n            headers = {\n                \"X-RateLimit-Limit\": str(limit),\n                \"X-RateLimit-Remaining\": str(max(0, limit - current)),\n                \"X-RateLimit-Reset\": str(int(time.time()) + window_seconds),\n            }\n\n            if not allowed:\n                headers[\"Retry-After\"] = str(retry_after)\n                response = jsonify({\n                    \"error\": \"rate_limit_exceeded\",\n                    \"message\": \"Too many requests. Please try again later.\",\n                    \"retry_after\": retry_after\n                })\n                response.status_code = 429\n                for h, v in headers.items():\n                    response.headers[h] = v\n                return response\n\n            response = f(*args, **kwargs)\n            for h, v in headers.items():\n                response.headers[h] = v\n            return response\n        return wrapped\n    return decorator\n\n# Apply rate limiting to endpoints\n@app.route('/api/v1/auth/login', methods=['POST'])\n@rate_limit(max_requests=5, window_seconds=60,\n            key_func=lambda: f\"ip:{request.remote_addr}\")\ndef login():\n    # Login logic\n    return jsonify({\"message\": \"Login successful\"})\n\n@app.route('/api/v1/users/me', methods=['GET'])\n@rate_limit(max_requests=60, window_seconds=60)\ndef get_profile():\n    # Profile logic\n    return jsonify({\"user\": \"data\"})\n\n@app.route('/api/v1/search', methods=['GET'])\n@rate_limit(max_requests=10, window_seconds=60)\ndef search():\n    # Search logic\n    return jsonify({\"results\": []})\n```\n\n### Step 3: Token Bucket Rate Limiter\n\n```python\nimport redis\nimport time\n\nclass TokenBucketRateLimiter:\n    \"\"\"Token bucket rate limiter allowing burst traffic within limits.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, redis_conn):\n        self.redis = redis_conn\n\n    def is_allowed(self, key, max_tokens, refill_rate, refill_interval=1):\n        \"\"\"\n        Token bucket algorithm:\n        - max_tokens: Maximum burst capacity\n        - refill_rate: Tokens added per refill_interval\n        - refill_interval: Seconds between refills\n        \"\"\"\n        now = time.time()\n        bucket_key = f\"tb:{key}\"\n\n        # Lua script for atomic token bucket operation\n        lua_script = \"\"\"\n        local key = KEYS[1]\n        local max_tokens = tonumber(ARGV[1])\n        local refill_rate = tonumber(ARGV[2])\n        local refill_interval = tonumber(ARGV[3])\n        local now = tonumber(ARGV[4])\n\n        local bucket = redis.call('hmget', key, 'tokens', 'last_refill')\n        local tokens = tonumber(bucket[1])\n        local last_refill = tonumber(bucket[2])\n\n        if tokens == nil then\n            tokens = max_tokens\n            last_refill = now\n        end\n\n        -- Refill tokens\n        local elapsed = now - last_refill\n        local refills = math.floor(elapsed / refill_interval)\n        if refills > 0 then\n            tokens = math.min(max_tokens, tokens + (refills * refill_rate))\n            last_refill = last_refill + (refills * refill_interval)\n        end\n\n        local allowed = 0\n        if tokens >= 1 then\n            tokens = tokens - 1\n            allowed = 1\n        end\n\n        redis.call('hmset', key, 'tokens', tokens, 'last_refill', last_refill)\n        redis.call('expire', key, math.ceil(max_tokens / refill_rate * refill_interval) + 10)\n\n        return {allowed, tokens, max_tokens}\n        \"\"\"\n\n        result = self.redis.eval(lua_script, 1, bucket_key,\n                                  max_tokens, refill_rate, refill_interval, now)\n        allowed = bool(result[0])\n        remaining = int(result[1])\n        limit = int(result[2])\n\n        return allowed, remaining, limit\n```\n\n### Step 4: Tiered Rate Limiting with User Plans\n\n```python\nfrom enum import Enum\n\nclass UserTier(Enum):\n    FREE = \"free\"\n    PREMIUM = \"premium\"\n    ENTERPRISE = \"enterprise\"\n\nTIER_LIMITS = {\n    UserTier.FREE: {\n        \"default\": (60, 60),          # 60 req/min\n        \"search\": (10, 60),           # 10 req/min\n        \"export\": (5, 3600),          # 5 req/hour\n        \"daily_total\": (1000, 86400), # 1000 req/day\n    },\n    UserTier.PREMIUM: {\n        \"default\": (300, 60),\n        \"search\": (50, 60),\n        \"export\": (20, 3600),\n        \"daily_total\": (10000, 86400),\n    },\n    UserTier.ENTERPRISE: {\n        \"default\": (1000, 60),\n        \"search\": (200, 60),\n        \"export\": (100, 3600),\n        \"daily_total\": (100000, 86400),\n    },\n}\n\ndef get_rate_limit_for_request(user_tier, endpoint_category=\"default\"):\n    \"\"\"Get rate limit configuration based on user tier and endpoint.\"\"\"\n    tier_config = TIER_LIMITS.get(user_tier, TIER_LIMITS[UserTier.FREE])\n    limit_config = tier_config.get(endpoint_category, tier_config[\"default\"])\n    return limit_config  # (max_requests, window_seconds)\n\nclass TieredRateLimitMiddleware:\n    \"\"\"Middleware that applies rate limits based on user subscription tier.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, app, redis_conn):\n        self.app = app\n        self.limiter = SlidingWindowRateLimiter(redis_conn)\n\n    def __call__(self, environ, start_response):\n        # Extract user info from request\n        user_id = environ.get(\"HTTP_X_USER_ID\")\n        user_tier = UserTier(environ.get(\"HTTP_X_USER_TIER\", \"free\"))\n        endpoint = environ.get(\"PATH_INFO\", \"/\")\n\n        # Determine endpoint category\n        category = \"default\"\n        if \"/search\" in endpoint:\n            category = \"search\"\n        elif \"/export\" in endpoint:\n            category = \"export\"\n\n        max_requests, window = get_rate_limit_for_request(user_tier, category)\n        key = f\"tiered:{user_id or environ.get('REMOTE_ADDR')}:{category}\"\n\n        allowed, current, limit, retry_after = self.limiter.is_allowed(\n            key, max_requests, window)\n\n        if not allowed:\n            status = \"429 Too Many Requests\"\n            headers = [\n                (\"Content-Type\", \"application/json\"),\n                (\"Retry-After\", str(retry_after)),\n                (\"X-RateLimit-Limit\", str(limit)),\n                (\"X-RateLimit-Remaining\", \"0\"),\n            ]\n            start_response(status, headers)\n            body = f'{{\"error\":\"rate_limit_exceeded\",\"retry_after\":{retry_after},\"tier\":\"{user_tier.value}\"}}'\n            return [body.encode()]\n\n        return self.app(environ, start_response)\n```\n\n### Step 5: Distributed Rate Limiting for Microservices\n\n```python\n# Centralized rate limiting service using Redis Cluster\nimport redis\nfrom redis.cluster import RedisCluster\n\nclass DistributedRateLimiter:\n    \"\"\"Rate limiter for microservice architectures using Redis Cluster.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self):\n        self.redis = RedisCluster(\n            startup_nodes=[\n                {\"host\": \"redis-node-1\", \"port\": 6379},\n                {\"host\": \"redis-node-2\", \"port\": 6379},\n                {\"host\": \"redis-node-3\", \"port\": 6379},\n            ],\n            decode_responses=True\n        )\n\n    def check_and_increment(self, service_name, user_id, endpoint,\n                             max_requests, window_seconds):\n        \"\"\"Atomic check-and-increment using Redis Lua script.\"\"\"\n        key = f\"rl:{{{service_name}}}:{user_id}:{endpoint}\"\n\n        # Lua script ensures atomicity across the check and increment\n        lua_script = \"\"\"\n        local key = KEYS[1]\n        local max_requests = tonumber(ARGV[1])\n        local window = tonumber(ARGV[2])\n        local now = tonumber(ARGV[3])\n        local window_start = now - window\n\n        -- Remove old entries\n        redis.call('zremrangebyscore', key, '-inf', window_start)\n\n        -- Count current entries\n        local count = redis.call('zcard', key)\n\n        if count >= max_requests then\n            -- Get oldest entry for retry-after calculation\n            local oldest = redis.call('zrange', key, 0, 0, 'WITHSCORES')\n            local retry_after = 0\n            if #oldest > 0 then\n                retry_after = math.ceil(tonumber(oldest[2]) + window - now)\n            end\n            return {0, count, retry_after}\n        end\n\n        -- Add new entry\n        redis.call('zadd', key, now, now .. ':' .. math.random(100000))\n        redis.call('expire', key, window + 1)\n\n        return {1, count + 1, 0}\n        \"\"\"\n\n        result = self.redis.eval(lua_script, 1, key,\n                                  max_requests, window_seconds, time.time())\n        return {\n            \"allowed\": bool(result[0]),\n            \"current\": int(result[1]),\n            \"retry_after\": int(result[2]),\n        }\n```\n\n## Key Concepts\n\n| Term | Definition |\n|------|------------|\n| **Sliding Window** | Rate limiting algorithm that tracks requests in a rolling time window, providing smoother rate enforcement than fixed windows |\n| **Token Bucket** | Algorithm where tokens are added at a fixed rate and consumed per request, allowing controlled bursts up to the bucket capacity |\n| **Fixed Window** | Simplest rate limiting where requests are counted per fixed time window (e.g., per minute), susceptible to burst at window boundaries |\n| **429 Too Many Requests** | HTTP status code indicating the client has exceeded the rate limit, accompanied by Retry-After header |\n/root/.codex/skills/implementing-api-rate-limiting-and-throttling/references/api-reference.md\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

The API must protect against abuse while providing fair access to paying customers. The API runs on 6 instances behind an AWS ALB.\n\n**Approach**:\n1. Deploy Redis Cluster (3 nodes) for distributed rate limit state\n2. Implement sliding window rate limiter using Redis sorted sets with Lua scripts for atomicity\n3. Configure per-tier limits: Free (60 req/min), Premium (300 req/min), Enterprise (1000 req/min)\n4. Add stricter limits on authentication endpoints (5 req/min per IP) regardless of tier\n5. Implement resource-intensive endpoint limits (search: 10 req/min free, export: 5 req/hour)\n6. Set rate limit response headers on every response (X-RateLimit-Limit, X-RateLimit-Remaining, X-RateLimit-Reset)\n7. Return 429 with Retry-After header and JSON error body when limits are exceeded\n8. Set up Prometheus metrics for rate limit hits and CloudWatch alarms for unusual patterns\n\n**Pitfalls**:\n- Using in-memory rate limiting without shared state across instances, allowing limit bypass by hitting different servers\n- Not implementing rate limiting on authentication endpoints separately from general API limits\n- Using fixed windows that allow burst at window boundaries (2x the limit in a short period)\n- Not including rate limit headers on successful responses, giving clients no visibility into their quota\n- Trusting X-Forwarded-For for IP identification without validating it against the load balancer\n\n## Output Format\n\n```\n## Rate Limiting Implementation Report\n\n**API**: Public API v2\n**Algorithm**: Sliding Window (Redis Sorted Sets)\n**Backend**: Redis Cluster (3 nodes)\n**Deployment**: 6 API instances behind AWS ALB\n\n### Rate Limit Configuration\n\n| Tier | Default | Search | Export | Auth (per IP) |\n|------|---------|--------|--------|---------------|\n| Free | 60/min | 10/min | 5/hour | 5/min |\n| Premium | 300/min | 50/min | 20/hour | 5/min |\n| Enterprise | 1000/min | 200/min | 100/hour | 10/min |\n\n### Validation Results (k6 load test)\n\n- Free tier: Rate limited at 61st request (correct)\n- Premium tier: Rate limited at 301st request (correct)\n- Cross-instance: Rate limiting consistent across all 6 instances\n- Redis failover: Rate limiting degrades gracefully (allows traffic) when Redis is unreachable\n- Retry-After header: Accurate within 1 second of actual reset time\n- Response overhead: < 2ms added latency per request for rate limit check\n```\n# API Reference: Implementing API Rate Limiting and Throttling\n\n## Token Bucket Algorithm\n\n```python\nimport time\nclass TokenBucket:\n    def __init__(self, capacity, refill_rate):\n        self.capacity = capacity\n        self.tokens = capacity\n        self.refill_rate = refill_rate  # tokens/sec\n        self.last_refill = time.time()\n\n    def allow(self):\n        now = time.time()\n        self.tokens = min(self.capacity,\n            self.tokens + (now - self.last_refill) * self.refill_rate)\n        self.last_refill = now\n        if self.tokens >= 1:\n            self.tokens -= 1\n            return True\n        return False\n```\n\n## Redis Sliding Window\n\n```python\nimport redis, time\nr = redis.Redis()\ndef check_rate(client_id, window=60, limit=100):\n    key = f\"rl:{client_id}\"\n    now = time.time()\n    pipe = r.pipeline()\n    pipe.zremrangebyscore(key, 0, now - window)\n    pipe.zadd(key, {str(now): now})\n    pipe.zcard(key)\n    pipe.expire(key, window)\n    _, _, count, _ = pipe.execute()\n    return count <= limit\n```\n\n## HTTP 429 Response Headers\n\n| Header | Value | Description |\n|--------|-------|-------------|\n| `Retry-After` | `30` | Seconds until retry |\n| `X-RateLimit-Limit` | `100` | Max requests |\n| `X-RateLimit-Remaining` | `0` | Remaining requests |\n| `X-RateLimit-Reset` | epoch | Reset timestamp |\n\n## Kong Rate Limiting Plugin\n\n```bash\ncurl -X POST http://localhost:8001/services/{id}/plugins \\\n  -d \"name=rate-limiting\" \\\n  -d \"config.minute=100\" \\\n  -d \"config.policy=redis\" \\\n  -d \"config.redis_host=redis\"\n```\n\n### References\n\n- Redis Rate Limiting: https://redis.io/glossary/rate-limiting/\n- IETF RateLimit Headers: https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-ietf-httpapi-ratelimit-headers/\n- Kong Rate Limiting: https://docs.konghq.com/hub/kong-inc/rate-limiting/\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

Code\n\nSearch JavaScript files, HTML source, and other client-side resources for exposed secrets.\n\n```bash\n# Download and search JavaScript files for secrets\ncurl -s \"https://target.example.com/\" | \\\n  grep -oP 'src=\"[^\"]*\\.js[^\"]*\"' | \\\n  grep -oP '\"[^\"]*\"' | tr -d '\"' | while read js; do\n    echo \"=== Scanning: $js ===\"\n    # Handle relative URLs\n    if [[ \"$js\" == /* ]]; then\n      curl -s \"https://target.example.com$js\"\n    else\n      curl -s \"$js\"\n    fi | grep -inE \\\n      \"(api[_-]?key|apikey|api[_-]?secret|aws[_-]?access|aws[_-]?secret|private[_-]?key|password|secret|token|auth|credential|AKIA[0-9A-Z]{16})\" \\\n      | head -20\ndone\n\n# Search for common secret patterns\ncurl -s \"https://target.example.com/static/app.js\" | grep -nP \\\n  \"(AIza[0-9A-Za-z-_]{35}|AKIA[0-9A-Z]{16}|sk-[a-zA-Z0-9]{48}|ghp_[a-zA-Z0-9]{36}|xox[bpsa]-[0-9a-zA-Z-]{10,})\"\n\n# Check source maps for exposed source code\ncurl -s \"https://target.example.com/static/app.js.map\" | head -c 500\n# Source maps may contain original source code with embedded secrets\n\n# Search HTML source for exposed data\ncurl -s \"https://target.example.com/\" | grep -inE \\\n  \"(api_key|secret|password|token|private_key|database_url|smtp_password)\" | head -20\n\n# Check for exposed .env or configuration files\nfor file in .env .env.local .env.production config.json settings.json \\\n  .aws/credentials .docker/config.json; do\n  status=$(curl -s -o /dev/null -w \"%{http_code}\" \\\n    \"https://target.example.com/$file\")\n  if [ \"$status\" == \"200\" ]; then\n    echo \"FOUND: $file ($status)\"\n  fi\ndone\n```\n\n### Step 2: Analyze API Responses for Data Over-Exposure\n\nCheck if API endpoints return more data than necessary.\n\n```bash\n# Fetch user profile and examine response fields\ncurl -s -H \"Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN\" \\\n  \"https://target.example.com/api/users/me\" | jq .\n\n# Look for sensitive fields that should not be exposed:\n# - password, password_hash, password_salt\n# - ssn, social_security_number, national_id\n# - credit_card_number, card_cvv, card_expiry\n# - api_key, secret_key, access_token, refresh_token\n# - internal_id, database_id\n# - ip_address, session_id\n# - date_of_birth, drivers_license\n\n# Check list endpoints for excessive data\ncurl -s -H \"Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN\" \\\n  \"https://target.example.com/api/users\" | jq '.[0] | keys'\n\n# Compare public vs authenticated responses\necho \"=== Public ===\"\ncurl -s \"https://target.example.com/api/users/1\" | jq 'keys'\necho \"=== Authenticated ===\"\ncurl -s -H \"Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN\" \\\n  \"https://target.example.com/api/users/1\" | jq 'keys'\n\n# Check error responses for information leakage\ncurl -s -X POST \\\n  -H \"Content-Type: application/json\" \\\n  -d '{\"invalid\": \"data\"}' \\\n  \"https://target.example.com/api/users\" | jq .\n# Look for: stack traces, database queries, internal paths, version info\n\n# Test for PII in search/autocomplete responses\ncurl -s -H \"Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN\" \\\n  \"https://target.example.com/api/search?q=john\" | jq .\n# May return full user records instead of just names\n```\n\n### Step 3: Test Data Transmission Security\n\nVerify that sensitive data is encrypted during transmission.\n\n```bash\n# Check TLS configuration\n# Using testssl.sh\n./testssl.sh \"https://target.example.com\"\n\n# Quick TLS checks with curl\ncurl -s -v \"https://target.example.com/\" 2>&1 | grep -E \"(SSL|TLS|cipher|subject)\"\n\n# Check for HTTP (non-HTTPS) endpoints\ncurl -s -I \"http://target.example.com/\" | head -5\n# Should redirect to HTTPS\n\n# Check for mixed content (HTTP resources on HTTPS pages)\ncurl -s \"https://target.example.com/\" | grep -oP \"http://[^\\\"'> ]+\" | head -20\n\n# Check if sensitive forms submit over HTTPS\ncurl -s \"https://target.example.com/login\" | grep -oP 'action=\"[^\"]*\"'\n# Form action should use HTTPS\n\n# Check for sensitive data in URL parameters (query string)\n# URLs are logged in browser history, server logs, proxy logs, Referer headers\n# Look for: /login?username=admin&password=secret\n# /api/data?ssn=123-45-6789\n# /search?credit_card=4111111111111111\n\n# Check WebSocket encryption\ncurl -s \"https://target.example.com/\" | grep -oP \"(ws|wss)://[^\\\"'> ]+\"\n# ws:// is unencrypted; should only use wss://\n```\n\n### Step 4: Examine Browser Storage for Sensitive Data\n\nCheck local storage, session storage, cookies, and cached responses.\n\n```bash\n# Check what cookies are set and their security attributes\ncurl -s -I \"https://target.example.com/login\" | grep -i \"set-cookie\"\n\n# In browser DevTools (Application tab):\n# 1. Local Storage: Check for stored tokens, PII, credentials\n# 2. Session Storage: Check for temporary sensitive data\n# 3. IndexedDB: Check for cached application data\n# 4. Cache Storage: Check for cached API responses containing PII\n# 5. Cookies: Check for sensitive data in cookie values\n\n# Common insecure storage patterns:\n# localStorage.setItem('access_token', 'eyJ...');  // XSS can steal\n# localStorage.setItem('user', JSON.stringify({email: '...', ssn: '...'}));\n# sessionStorage.setItem('credit_card', '4111...');\n\n# Check for autocomplete on sensitive forms\ncurl -s \"https://target.example.com/login\" | \\\n  grep -oP '<input[^>]*(password|credit|ssn|card)[^>]*>' | \\\n  grep -v 'autocomplete=\"off\"'\n# Password and credit card fields should have autocomplete=\"off\"\n\n# Check Cache-Control headers on sensitive pages\nfor page in /account/profile /api/users/me /transactions /billing; do\n  echo -n \"$page: \"\n  curl -s -I \"https://target.example.com$page\" \\\n    -H \"Authorization: Bearer $TOKEN\" | \\\n    grep -i \"cache-control\" | tr -d '\\r'\n  echo\ndone\n# Sensitive pages should have: Cache-Control: no-store\n```\n\n### Step 5: Scan Git Repositories and Source Code for Secrets\n\nSearch for accidentally committed secrets in version control.\n\n```bash\n# Check for exposed .git directory\ncurl -s \"https://target.example.com/.git/config\"\ncurl -s
